## 5. Topic discovery method
The topic engine starts with a bounded recent PubMed primary-study sample, supplements it with configured-journal coverage, and ranks clinically specific concepts rather than raw seed frequency. Each candidate is checked in separate RCT and observational searches, with dedicated observational prognosis, incidence/prevalence and predictor/risk-factor tracks plus broad and focused review searches. These are discovery signals, not exhaustive searches, eligible-study counts or proof of novelty.

**Recent primary-study seed sample:** 567 records.
**Candidates assessed:** 18.

### Citation-level screening exports
CSV files are preliminary triage aids. Records labelled unclear/manual_check require human title/abstract review; no citation is declared finally eligible without a protocol and full-text assessment. Separate blank outcome compatibility worksheets accompany each primary-study track; complete them from full texts before deciding poolability.

**Score formula (heuristic, not validated):** evidence-volume signal min(70, 16 × log2(search hits + 1)) plus a capped seed-support signal. Broad review-hit volume is not subtracted as a novelty penalty because topic-only overlap is not a redundancy decision. Exact/close overlap must be adjudicated by comparing review PICO, dates, outcomes and included studies.
**Evidence-signal label:** at least 3 RCT-search hits or 5 broad observational-search hits triggers human screening. Review-search hits are reported as broad and focused overlap signals but do not automatically reject a candidate. This is not proof that records are eligible, novel or poolable.

## 6. Ranked topics for RCT-only SRMA consideration

### 1. Glucagon-Like Peptide-1: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials
**Candidate source:** title/abstract  
**Seed records sharing concepts:** 3  
**Score:** 79/100 (heuristic triage, not a validated model)  
**Decision signal:** EVIDENCE SIGNAL — screen primary records; assess review overlap manually

> **Interpretation:** PubMed hit counts are candidate retrieval counts, not screened or eligible studies. RCT and observational searches are separate and can overlap. Sample sizes are abstract-only signals and must not be summed because cohorts may overlap. Broad and focused review-search hits are overlap signals, not proof of duplication. PROSPERO overlap and outcome compatibility have not been adjudicated. Prognosis, incidence/prevalence and predictor searches are separate observational question tracks; their counts are retrieval signals only.

> **Interpretation:** PubMed hit counts are candidate retrieval counts, not screened or eligible studies. RCT and observational searches are separate and can overlap. Sample sizes are abstract-only signals and must not be summed because cohorts may overlap. Broad and focused review-search hits are overlap signals, not proof of duplication. PROSPERO overlap and outcome compatibility have not been adjudicated. Prognosis, incidence/prevalence and predictor searches are separate observational question tracks; their counts are retrieval signals only.

## 8. Important interpretation rules
- RCT and observational evidence counts are separate search results. Do not combine them into one pooled estimate.
- Prospective cohorts, retrospective cohorts, case-control and cross-sectional studies remain distinct in the underlying metadata and are listed separately in the new-study feed.
- PubMed counts are candidate hits, not the number of included studies. Screen every citation against prespecified eligibility criteria.
- Abstract sample-size snippets are not summed: cohorts may overlap and abstracts may report different denominators.
- A review hit is an overlap warning, not proof that the proposed PICO is already covered. Read its population, dates, interventions, outcomes and included studies.
- Check PROSPERO and other registries manually; a search link is provided but registration status is not programmatically adjudicated.
- No automatic impact factor, Q1/Q2, acceptance probability or publication guarantee is assigned.

## 9. Next human steps
1. Export each shortlisted RCT and observational search separately to RIS/CSV.
2. Screen RCT, cohort, case-control and cross-sectional records separately; do not merge designs to inflate counts.
3. Retrieve full texts; confirm population, design, interventions/exposures, comparators, outcomes, follow-up and overlap.
4. Assess design-appropriate risk of bias and whether outcomes/timepoints are sufficiently compatible for pooling.
5. Check PROSPERO and recent reviews before selecting/registering a final protocol.
